Source

Your dreams might show you all kinds of insights into things that you didn't realise you were thinking about. Common dreams like being able to fly or falling, your teeth falling out or having no clothes on in a public place probably mean something similar in most people. But the key to understanding exactly what they mean to you is to connect them to the events and feelings in your daily life.

Reference 1

People have different dreams when they sleep. Some common dreams are flying, falling, losing teeth, or being outside with no clothes. These dreams often mean the same thing for many people. To know what your dream means, think about what is happening in your life and how you feel.

Reference 2

Dreams can tell us something about our thoughts and feelings, even if we don't realise it. Many people have similar dreams, like flying, falling, losing teeth, or being in public without clothes. These dreams often have the same meaning for most people. To really understand your dream, you should think about your daily life and emotions.

Prediction

Your dreams can give you insights into thoughts you weren't aware of. Many people have common dreams, like flying or falling, losing teeth, or being naked in public. To understand what these dreams mean for you, link them to your daily life events and feelings.

One of the most interesting cases of wild animals living in a city are the wild dogs of Moscow. In Moscow, there are approximately 35,000 wild dogs living on the streets. Some of the dogs were born wild, while others are pets that have been abandoned by their owners. Some dogs live alone and others live in packs. In 2010, scientists studied the dogs and found that the dogs have adapted remarkably successfully to urban life. They have learned that it is safer to cross the street with people and some dogs appear to understand traffic lights.

Reference 1

There are many wild dogs in Moscow, about 35,000. Some were born wild, and some were pets but left by their owners. Some live alone and others live in groups. In 2010, scientists studied the dogs. They found that the dogs have learned how to live in the city. Some dogs wait and cross the street with people. A few dogs even seem to know traffic lights.

Reference 2

In 2010, scientists studied them and found that the dogs have learned to live well in the city. They often cross the street with people, and some even seem to understand traffic lights.

Wonder has been a bestseller in the United States and has already won several awards. Many schools are using it as a starting point for discussions about bullying, friendship and what it's like to be different. A film version is being made, and many, including me, are waiting eagerly to see what RJ Palacio writes next.

Wonder is the first book by RJ Palacio, a writer from New York. She got the idea after seeing a girl with a face that looked different. The book is very popular in the United States and has won many awards. Many schools use the book to talk about bullying, friendship, and being different. A movie is now being made, and many people want to know what RJ Palacio will write next.

Reference 2

Wonder is the first novel by RJ Palacio, who comes from New York. She says that she got the idea for the story after seeing a girl with a facial difference on the street. The book became a bestseller in the United States and has already won several awards. Many schools use it to start conversations about bullying, friendship, and how it feels to be different. Source

Chloe has two pet rats, Zena and Roxy. Chloe knows they're not typical pets and she has lost count of the times she's patiently tried to explain to people that rats aren't dirty and they make fantastic pets if you look after them correctly. But she understands why people react badly. She didn't like rats either until her friend Martin showed her his pet rats and she saw how affectionate and intelligent they were. Last year, when one of Martin's rats had babies, Chloe decided she'd like to have two of them.

Reference 1

Chloe has two pet rats, called Zena and Roxy. She knows that rats are not usual pets, and many people think they are dirty. Chloe often tells people that rats can be very good pets if you take care of them. She didn't like rats before, but her friend Martin showed her his rats. She saw they were kind and clever. Last year, one of Martin's rats had babies. Chloe chose two of them to keep.

Reference 2

Chloe owns two pet rats named Zena and Roxy. She knows rats are not common pets, and she has tried many times to explain that rats are not dirty and can be great pets if you care for them properly. She understands why people are unsure about rats: she didn't like them either until her friend Martin showed her his. After seeing how loving and smart they were, she changed her mind. When one of Martin's rats had babies last year, Chloe decided to keep two of them.

Prediction

Chloe has two pet rats, Zena and Roxy. She knows people often misunderstand rats as pets. They might think rats are dirty, but Chloe knows that's not true. With proper care, rats can be loving and smart companions. Chloe didn't always like rats herself until her friend Martin showed her his pet rats. After seeing how affectionate and intelligent they were, she changed her mind. Last year, when Martin's rats had babies, Chloe decided to take two home as pets.

Source

Cape Town can be a dangerous place for baboons. Some have been injured or killed in car accidents and others have been electrocuted as they played with electric cables. Baboons are an endangered species and to save them and minimise conflicts between humans and baboons, the city council has employed a team of Baboon Monitors. The monitors' job is to chase baboons out of urban areas or catch them and release them in the countryside. This policy has had some success, though it is proving hard to stop baboons from coming back to the city once they see it as a source of easy food.

Reference 1

Cape Town can be dangerous for baboons. Some are hurt or die in car accidents. Some die from playing with electric wires. Baboons are animals that are in danger of disappearing. To help them, the city now has a team called Baboon Monitors. These people try to move baboons out of the city and take them back to the countryside. This plan works a little, but the baboons often come back because they find food in the city.

Reference 2

Cape Town is a risky place for baboons. Some are injured or killed by cars, and others are electrocuted when they touch electric wires. Because baboons are an endangered species, the city has hired a group called Baboon Monitors. Their job is to keep baboons out of the city by chasing them away or catching them and releasing them in the countryside. This plan has worked somewhat, but it's difficult to stop the baboons from returning since they see the city as a place to find easy food.

Prediction

Cape Town can be risky for baboons. Some have been hurt or killed in car accidents and others have been electrocuted while playing with electric cables. Baboons are an endangered species, and to protect them and reduce conflicts between humans and baboons, the city council has hired a Baboon Monitor team. The monitors' job is to drive baboons out of urban areas or catch and release them in the countryside. This plan has worked to some extent, but it's been difficult to keep baboons from returning to the city when they see it as an easy food source.

I work for a global IT company, but because their headquarters is in the States, I do all my work online from home. That means that I don't waste time commuting or making idle chit-chat with colleagues. I work on a project basis, and this flexibility is very valuable to me because it means that I can easily take some time off when my children need me to go to their school performances or if I need to schedule an appointment with the dentist. The downside is that without clear office hours, I tend to work well into the evening, sometimes skipping dinner to finish a task.

Reference 1

I work for a large IT company, but I work from home because the office is in the USA. I do everything online. This is good because I don't waste time going to work or talking too much with coworkers. I work on different projects, and this is helpful. I can take time off if my children have a school show or if I need to see the dentist. But there is a problem: I don't have fixed hours, so I often work late and sometimes skip dinner to finish my work.

Reference 2

I work for a global IT company, but since the main office is in the United States, I do all of my work from home online. This is helpful because I save time: no travel and no small talk with coworkers. I work on different projects, and I really like the flexibility. I can take time off if my children have something at school or if I need to go to the dentist. However, there is a disadvantage. Without clear office hours, I often work late into the evening and sometimes miss dinner just to finish a task.

Prediction

"I work for a company in the IT industry that operates on a global scale. My job is done from home because the company's main office is in the United States. This setup saves me time spent on commuting and unnecessary small talk with colleagues. I like the flexibility of working on projects, as it allows me to take time off when my children have school events or if I need to see the dentist. However, without fixed office hours, I often find myself working late into the night, sometimes missing dinner to complete a task."

In South America, many street artists went to work in poor areas adding colour, life and beauty to grey, city walls.

Reference 2

Some artists used street art to talk about politics and serious issues like war, inequality, and discrimination. Others focused on making beautiful and interesting art. In South America, many street artists painted in poor neighbourhoods to bring colour and beauty to the grey walls of the cities.

Prediction

In the 1990s, graffiti was still present, but there was also a rise in street art worldwide. Some artists created works to raise political awareness about issues like war, inequality, and discrimination. Other street artists focused on creating beautiful, appealing images. In South America, many artists painted in poor areas, adding color, vibrancy, and beauty to gray city walls.

Sam squinted against the sun at the distant dust trail raked up by the car on its way up to the Big House. The horses kicked and flicked their tails at flies, not caring about their owner's first visit in ten months. Sam waited. Mr Carter didn't come out here unless he had to, which was just fine by Sam. The more he kept out of his boss's way, the longer he'd have a job. Mr, Carter wiped the sweat from the back of his neck. The damp patches on his shirt drew together like shapes in an atlas. His skin was already turning ruddy in the June sun. Otherwise he had the indoor tan of a man that made money while other people did the work.

His skin was red from the hot June sun. Normally, he stayed inside and made money while others worked.

Many city residents in Berlin, Germany complained that shooting pigs was unnecessarily cruel and there were heated arguments between hunters and animal lovers. In response to protests, the city council decided to take more peaceful action against pigs. Instead of shooting them, they have put up fences and made it illegal to feed the pigs. Reference 1

An important inspiration for the Hunger Games book comes from Suzanne's love of classical Greek mythology. When she was a child, one of her favourite stories was Theseus and the Minotaur. In this story the city of Athens is ruled by Crete, but the Athenians rebel. As a punishment, Minos the king of Crete forces the Athenians to send seven boys and seven girls to Crete every year. The youths are thrown into the labyrinth and eaten by a monster that lives there called the Minotaur. But one of the youths, named Theseus, fights the Minotaur and kills it. Suzanne sees Katniss Everdeen as a futuristic Theseus.

Reference 1

The writer of The Hunger Games, Suzanne, got the idea from old Greek stories. When she was young, she liked the story of Theseus and the Minotaur. In the story, the city of Athens must send children to a monster called the Minotaur every year. The Minotaur lives in a maze and eats the children. One boy, Theseus, fights the monster and kills it. Suzanne thinks Katniss, the main character in The Hunger Games, is like Theseus, but in the future.

Reference 2

One big inspiration for The Hunger Games came from Suzanne Collins' love of ancient Greek myths. As a child, one of her favourite stories was Theseus and the Minotaur. In the story, Athens is controlled by Crete, and every year the king of Crete, Minos, makes the Athenians send seven boys and seven girls. These children are sent into a maze where a monster, the Minotaur, lives. Theseus, one of the children, kills the monster. Suzanne sees her character Katniss as a modern version of Theseus.

Prediction

Suzanne sees Katniss Everdeen as a futuristic version of Theseus.

A different trend is springing up in opposition to consumerism – the 'buy nothing' trend. The idea originated in Canada in the early 1990s and then moved to the US, where it became a rejection of the overspending and overconsumption of Black Friday and Cyber Monday during Thanksgiving weekend. On Buy Nothing Day people organise various types of protests and cut up their credit cards. Throughout the year, Buy Nothing groups organise the exchange and repair of items they already own.

Reference 1

Some people are starting a new idea called the 'buy nothing' trend. It began in Canada in the 1990s and then went to the USA. These people don't like buying too much on days like Black Friday and Cyber Monday. On 'Buy Nothing Day', people protest and cut their credit cards. During the year, they also fix and trade things instead of buying new ones.

Reference 2

A new movement against consumerism is growing – it's called the 'buy nothing' trend. It started in Canada in the early 1990s and then spread to the US. People involved in this movement want to avoid overspending, especially during shopping events like Black Friday and Cyber Monday. On Buy Nothing Day, they hold protests and even cut up their credit cards. Throughout the year, they also organise activities to repair or exchange used items instead of buying new ones.

Prediction

A new trend is appearing as a reaction to consumerism - the "buy nothing" trend. This idea started in Canada in the early 1990s and then spread to the US, where it became a way for people to reject the over spending and consuming that happens on Black Friday and Cyber Monday during Thanksgiving weekend. On Buy Nothing Day, people hold different types of protests and cut up their credit cards. Throughout the year, Buy Nothing groups help people exchange and fix items they already have.

If most people have four to six dreams every night after the age of ten, that's as many as 2,000 dreams per year. So, by the time they reach 80 years old the average person might have had 140,000 dreams. Even if we forget 95–99 per cent of our dreams, that's still a few thousand remembered dreams across a lifetime. It's not too difficult to believe that, by coincidence, a dream event is followed by a real-life event that's similar to it, especially if the subject of the dream is something that happens often in everyday life.

Around the 18th and 19th centuries two main ideas about dreams became popular. One was the idea that the things we see in our dreams represent things hidden from our conscious mind. Freud believed that if we understand what dreams are telling us, we can understand our hidden feelings and the problems they cause. However, the other theory was the opposite. It said that dreams are just a collection of random activity in the brain while it's organising memories and events from the day. When we wake up, we try to make sense of these random images and feelings and put them into some sort of story.

Reference 1

In the 18th and 19th centuries, people had two main ideas about dreams. The first idea was that dreams show things that we dont know in our mind. Freud believed that if we look at dreams, we can understand our hidden feelings and problems. The second idea was opposite. It said dreams are only random things in the brain when it is cleaning and organising the day's memories. When we wake up, we try to make a story from these pics and feelings.

Reference 2

In the 18th and 19th centuries, two main ideas about dreams became popular. The first was that dreams show things that are hidden in our minds. Freud thought that by studying dreams, we can learn about our secret feelings and the problems they create. The second idea was the opposite. It said that the dreams are the result of random brain activity while it is sorting memories and events from the day. When we wake up, our brain tries to connect these random images and feelings into a story.

Prediction

It said that dreams are just a mix of random brain activity while it sorts out memories and events from the day. When we wake up, we try to make sense of these random images and feelings and turn them into some kind of story.

If NASA gets the money, it could finish this in 10 years. After that, the team must still plan how to stop the very dangerous asteriods, called PHAs, from hitting Earth.

NASA can only find the rest of the middle-sized asteriods if it gets money to build NEOCam, a space telescope with a 0.5-meter lens that uses infrared light to find them. With enough money, NEOCam could probably finish this work in 10 years. But after that, the planetary defence team would still have to think about how to protect Earth from the most dangerous asteroids, called PHAs.

Prediction

Whether NASA can find the rest of the middle-sized near-Earth objects (NEOs) depends on getting funding for NEOCam. NEOCam is a 0.5-meter space telescope that uses infrared light to locate asteroids. If it gets the money, NASA could probably find all the remaining NEOs in ten years. After finding them, the planetary defense team would still need to figure out how to protect Earth from dangerous asteroids, also known as potentially hazardous asteroids (PHAs).

The 'buy nothing' trend has now reached influencers on social media who usually share posts of clothing and make-up that they recommend for people to buy. Some YouTube stars now encourage their viewers not to buy anything at all for periods as long as a year. Two friends in Canada spent a year working towards buying only food. For the first three months they learned how to live without buying electrical goods, clothes or things for the house. For the next stage, they gave up services, for example haircuts, eating out at restaurants or buying petrol for their cars. In one year, they'd saved $55,000.

Reference 1

In the first three months, they stopped buying electronics, clothes, and things for the house. Later, they also stopped paying for services, like haircuts, eating in restaurants, and petrol for their cars. In one year, they saved $55,000.

Some people think writing your dreams down can open your mind to lucid dreaming. Lucid dreams are dreams where you're not only aware you're dreaming but you can make decisions to affect the events of your dreams. So, maybe you can turn a nightmare into a safe-feeling dream, or you can direct the plot of your dream like a film director. Then, instead of just working out the meaning of your dreams, you'll be creating them, though you might want to continue having some dreams that allow your subconscious to tell you what's on your mind!

Reference 1

Some people think that writing down dreams in a notebook can help them have lucid dreams. A lucid dream is when you know you are dreaming, and you can change what happens. For example, you can make a bad dream feel safe, or you can control the story like a movie director. In this way, you are not only looking for the meaning of your dreams, but also making them. But sometimes, you may still want dreams that show what is in your mind.

Reference 2

Some people think that writing down their dreams can help them experience lucis dreaming. A lucid dream is a dream where you know you are dreaming and can make choices that change what happens. For instance,  you might turn a nightmare into a dream that feels safe, or you could guide the story of the dream like a film director. This means you are not only trying to understand your dreams but also creating them. Still, you may want to keep some dreams that show what your subconscious mind is thinking.

Prediction

Some people believe that writing down your dreams can help you start having lucid dreams. Lucid dreams are dreams where you know you're dreaming and can control what happens in them. This means you could change a bad dream into a pleasant one or guide the story of your dream like a movie director. Instead of just trying to understand your dreams, you would be creating them. However, it might still be good to have some dreams where your subconscious mind shares its thoughts with you.

People might not realise they are part of the disposable clothing problem because they donate their unwanted clothes to charities. But charity shops can't sell all those unwanted clothes. 'Fast fashion' goes out of fashion as quickly as it came in and is often too poor quality to recycle; people don't want to buy it second-hand. Huge quantities end up being thrown away, and a lot of clothes that charities can't sell are sent abroad, causing even more economic and environmental problems.

Reference 1

Some people don't know they are part of the clothing waste problem because they give old clothes to charities. But charity shops cannot sell all of them. Fast fashion quickly goes out of style and is often poor in quality, so people don't want to buy second-hand. A lot of clothes are thrown away, and many clothes that charities cannot sell are sent to other countries. This causes money and environmental problems.

Reference 2

People don't realise they are part of the problem of disposable clothing because they give their unwanted clothes to charity shops. However, these shops cannot sell everything. Fast fashion quickly goes out of style and is often too low in quality to recycle, so people don't want to buy it second-hand. As a result, a large amount of clothes are thrown away, and many that charities cannot sell are sent to other countries, which creates more economic and environmental problems.

Prediction

Fast fashion becomes outdated quickly and is often poor quality, making it unsuitable for recycling. People usually don't want to buy such items second-hand. As a result, large quantities of clothing end up in landfills.

Additionally, many clothes that charity shops can't sell are sent to other countries, causing further economic and environmental issues.

Source

In her previous work environment, Gabriela was used to a high power distance culture where power and authority are respected and everyone has their rightful place. In such a culture, leaders make the big decisions and are not often challenged. Her Swedish team, however, were used to working in a low power distance culture where subordinates often work together with their bosses to find solutions and make decisions. Here, leaders act as coaches or mentors who encourage independent thought and expect to be challenged.

Reference 1

In her old job, Gabriela worked in a place with high power distance. This means people respect authority, and leaders make the big decisions. Workers do not usually question their bosses. But her Swedish team worked in a low power distance culture. Here, workers and bosses make decisions together. Leaders act like coaches or teachers, and they want workers to think for themselves and even question them.

Reference 2

In her old job, Gabriela worked in a culture with high power distance. In this culture, people respect power and authority, and everyone has a clear place. Leaders make the big decisions, and others do not usually question them. But her Swedish team was used to a low power distance culture. In this culture, employees often work with their bosses to solve problems and make decisions together. Leaders act more like coaches or mentors, encouraging independent thinking and expecting to be challenged.

Prediction

Gabriela was used to a work environment where power and authority are respected, and leaders make decisions without being questioned. This is called a high power distance culture. In her Swedish team, things were different. They were used to a low power distance culture where subordinates and bosses work together to find solutions and make decisions. Here, leaders act as coaches or mentors who encourage independent thought and expect to be challenged. Gabriela had to adapt to this new culture.

When Gabriela became aware of the cultural differences between her and her team, she took the initiative to have an open conversation with them about their feelings about her leadership. Pleased to be asked for their thoughts, Gabriela's team openly expressed that they were not used to being told what to do. They enjoyed having more room for initiative and creative freedom. When she told her team exactly what she needed them to do, they felt that she didn't trust them to do their job well. They realised that Gabriela was taking it personally when they tried to challenge or make changes to her decisions, and were able to explain that it was how they'd always worked.

Reference 1

When Gabriela saw that she and her team had cultural differences, she chose to talk with them openly about her leadership. The team was glad to be asked and said they were not used to being told what to do. They liked having more freedom and space to be creative. When Gabriela told them exactly what to do, they felt she didn't trust them to do their job. They also saw that Gabriela felt upset when they questioned her decisions. They explained that this was just the way they normally worked.

People have been painting and writing on walls throughout history. In prehistoric times, people in Africa and Europe used to paint pictures of animals and people in caves. Graffiti has been found on buildings at ancient sites in Greece, Italy, Syria and Iraq. In the Roman town of Pompeii, archaeologists have found numerous examples of graffiti written in Latin. These include religious, political and romantic messages, and even some magic spells!

Reference 1

People have painted and written on walls for a very long time. In early times, people in Africa and Europe painted animals and people inside caves. These messages were about religion, politics, love, and even magic spells!

Reference 2

People have painted and written on walls in history. In prehistory, people in Africa and Europe painted animals and people on the walls of caves. Graffiti has also been found on buildings at ancient sites in Greece, Italy, Syria, and Iraq. In the Roman town of Pompeii, archaeologists found many examples of graffiti in Latin. These messages included religious, political, and romantic topics, and even some magic spells!

Prediction

People have painted and written on walls for a long time. In ancient times, people in Africa and Europe drew pictures of animals and people in caves. Graffiti has been found on buildings in Greece, Italy, Syria, and Iraq. In the Roman town of Pompeii, archaeologists discovered many writings in Latin on walls. These include religious, political messages, love notes, and even some magic spells!

The first is DART – the Double Asteroid Redirection Test. Plans are scheduled to test DART on the moon of an asteroid called Didymos. 'Didymoon' is 150 metres wide, orbiting its 800-metre mother, and hopefully the impact of DART will knock it out of its orbit enough for Earth-based telescopes to pick up.

Reference 1

The first project is DART- the Double Asteroid Redirection Test. It will be tested on the moon of an asteroid called Didymos. This moon is called "Didymoon." It is 150 metres wide and goes around its bigger "mother" asteroid, which is 800 metres wide. Scientists hope that when DART hits Didymoon, it will change its path enough for telescopes on Earth to see.

Reference 2

The first project is called DART-the Double Asteroid Redirection Test. Scientists plan to test it on the moon of an asteroid called Didymos. This moon, called "Didymoon," is 150 metres wide and goes around its larger parent asteroid, which is 800 metres wide. The hope is that the impact of DART will move Didymoon slightly out of its orbit so that telescopes on Earth can detect the change.

Prediction

Didymoon, the moon of Didymos, is 150 meters wide and orbits its 800-meter mother. acronym_without_detected_expansion_candidate

Source

In 2010, the planetary defence team at NASA had identified and logged 90 per cent of the asteroids near Earth measuring 1km wide. These 'near-Earth objects', or NEOs, are the size of mountains and include anything within 50 million kilometres of Earth's orbit. With an estimated 50 left to log, NASA says none of the 887 it knows about are a significant danger to the planet.

Reference 1

In 2010, NASA found 90% of the big asteroids near Earth that are 1km wide. They are callled 'near-Earth objects'. They are very large like mountains and they move close to Earth's path. NASA thinks about 50 are still missing, but the 887 already found are not dangerous to Earth.

Reference 2

In 2010, the planetary defence team at NASA had found and recorded 90% of the asteroids near Earth that are 1km wide. These are called 'near-Earth objects'. They are as big as mountains and are found within 50 km of Earth's prbit. NASA believes there are about 50 more to find, but says that none of the 887 already known are a serious threat to our planet.

Prediction

In 2010, NASA's planetary defense team had identified and recorded 90% of the space rocks close to Earth that measure 1km wide. These 'near-Earth objects', or NEOs, are as big as mountains and are located within 50 million kilometers of Earth's orbit. NASA estimates that there are 50 NEOs left to log, but none of the 887 they know about pose a significant threat to Earth.

But maybe there was even more trouble for someone who knew nothing about horses and wanted to get away from his father.

Either way, metro passengers have now got so used to the sight of dogs on the trains that they travel largely unnoticed by locals.

In Britain, the average person spends more than £1,000 on new clothes a year, which is around four per cent of their income. That might not sound like much, but that figure hides two far more worrying trends for society and for the environment. First, a lot of that consumer spending is via credit cards. British people currently owe approximately £670 per adult to credit card companies. That's 66 per cent of the average wardrobe budget. Also, not only are people spending money they don't have, they're using it to buy things they don't need. Britain throws away 300,000 tons of clothing a year, most of which goes into landfill sites.

Source

Businesses have caught on to the profitability of the sharing economy and are seeking to gain from making use of those underutilised resources. A business model that has rapidly risen in popularity sees companies providing an online platform that puts customers in contact with those who can provide a particular product or service. Companies like Airbnb act as a middleman for people to cash in on their unused rooms and houses and let them out as lucrative accommodation. Another example is Uber, which encourages people to use their own personal cars as taxis to make some extra cash in their free time.

Reference 1

Many businesses know they can make money from the sharing economy. This means using things that people are not using all the time. One idea is to make a website or app to connect people who can provide a service. For example, Airbnb helps people rent out their free rooms or houses to travellers. Another example is Uber. It lets people use their own cars as taxis and earn some extra cash.

Reference 2

Businesses now see that the sharing economy can make a lot of money. They want to use resources that are not being fully used. A common business model is when companies create an online platform that connects customers with people who can offer a product or service. For example, Airbnb acts as a middleman that helps people rent out their empty rooms and houses to earn money. Another example is Uber, which lets people use their personal cars as taxis to make extra income in their free time.

Prediction

Businesses see the value in the sharing economy. They want to benefit from underused resources. A popular business model is providing an online platform. This platform connects customers with people who can offer a specific product or service.

For example, Airbnb lets people rent out their unused rooms or houses for money. Uber allows individuals to use their personal cars as taxis for extra cash in their free time.

Source

One of the most important modern functions of zoos is supporting international breeding programmes, particularly for endangered species. In the wild, some of the rarest species have difficulty in finding mates and breeding, and they might also be threatened by poachers, loss of their habitat and predators. A good zoo will enable these species to live and breed in a secure environment. In addition, as numbers of some wild species drop, there is an increased danger of populations becoming too genetically similar. Breeding programmes provide a safeguard: zoo-bred animals can be released into the wild to increase genetic diversity.

Reference 1

Zoos has an important role. They help animals that are endangered, which means very few in the world, by running breeding programmes. In the wild, rare animals often cannot find a mate to have babies. They can also be killed by hunters, lose their homes, and be eaten by other animals. In a good zoo, they can live safely and have babies. When the number of wild animals gets very small, they can become too similar in their genes. Breeding in zoos helps this. Later, the young animals can go back to the wild and make the group stronger.

Reference 2

One of the most important roles of zoos is supporting international breeding programmes, especially for animals that are endangered. In the wild, some rare animals find it hard to find partners and have babies. They are also in danger from hunters, the loss of their homes, and other animals that hunt them. A good zoo gives these species a safe place to live and reproduce. Another problem is that when the number of animals in the wild becomes very small, they can become too genetically similar. Breeding programmes help with this, because animals born in zoos can later be relaesed into the wild to make the population stronger and more diverse.

Prediction

1. Zoos have an important role in modern times. They support international breeding programs, especially for endangered species.
2. In the wild, rare species struggle to find mates and breed. They also face threats from poachers, loss of habitat, and predators.
3. A good zoo lets these species live and breed securely.
4. As some wild animal populations decrease, they can become too similar genetically.
5. Breeding programs offer protection: zoo-bred animals can be released into the wild to increase genetic diversity.

Source

Both of these kinds of accidents are extremely rare, but boats and trains can cause a bridge to collapse for different reasons. With trains, it's the velocity of the impact that can bring a bridge down. With boats, it's the very large mass they have that can bring about the collapse, even if they are moving very slowly when it occurs. The best way to avoid bridge failures is to plan for them. Modern technologies that can detect structural weakness, safer working environments and better designs can all help to reduce these terrible accidents.

Reference 1

Accidents with bridges do not happen often. But sometimes trains or boats can make a bridge fall. A fast train crash can break a bridge. A boat is very heavy, so even a slow boat can make a bridge collapse. The best way to stop this is to plan good. New technology can find weak parts of a bridge. Safer building work and better designs can also help prevent these accidents.

Reference 2

Both of these kinds of accidents are extremely rare, but boats and trains can cause a bridge collapse in different ways. For trains, the speed of the crash can break the bridge. For boats, it is their heavy weight that can cause the collapse, even if they are moving slowly. The best way to stop bridge failures is to prepare for them. Modern technology that finds weakness in bridges, safer working environments and better bridge designs can all help reduce these serious accidents.

Prediction

Both boat and train accidents that cause bridge collapses are rare. However, they happen for different reasons. With trains, it's usually the impact's speed that brings down a bridge. With boats, it's their large size that can lead to collapse, even if they're moving slowly.

To prevent bridge failures, we should plan ahead. Modern technologies that detect structural weakness, safer workplaces, and better designs can help reduce these terrible accidents.

Source

Psychologist and 'growth mindset' proponent Carol Dweck spoke of the plasticity of the brain and our ability to develop skills and talents that we might not have been good at to start with. Many of us tend to focus our praise on the end result and seemingly innate talents, e.g. 'You really have an eye for details' or 'You have a real talent for organising events'. However, research suggests that by focusing on the process of how things are done – praising effort, experimentation and problem-solving strategies – we can encourage the development of new skills and the continued honing of talents.

Reference 1

Psychologist Carol Dweck talks about the 'growth mindset'. This means the brain can change, and we can learn skills even if we are not good at them at the start. Often, people give praise only for the final result or for natural talent. For example: "You notice small details well" or "You are good at organising events." But research shows it is better to praise the way people work. Saying something like "Good effort" or "Good for trying new ideas" helps people learn new skills and improve their talents.

Reference 2

Psychologist Carol Dweck, who supports the idea of a 'growth mindset', spoke about how the brain can change and how we can develop skills and talents, even if we were not good at them at firstt. Many people usually give praise for the final result or for natural talent, for example: "You are good at noticing details" or "You are great at organising events." But research says that praising the process, like effort, trying new things, and solving problems, can help us develop new skills and get better at what we're already good at.

Early in her career, the psychologist Carol Dweck of Stanford University gave a group of ten-year-olds problems that were slightly too hard for them. One group reacted positively, said they loved challenge and understood that their abilities could be developed. She says they had a 'growth mindset' and are focused on what they can achieve in the future. But another group of children felt that their intelligence was being judged and they had failed. They had a 'fixed mindset' and were unable to imagine improving. Some of these children said they might cheat in the future; others looked for someone who had done worse than them to boost their self-esteem.

Reference 1

They thought the problems showed they were not smart, and they could not imagine improving. Dweck called this a 'fixed mindset'. Some children with this mindset said they might cheat next time, and others tried to feel better by looking at kids who did worse than they.

The majority of people believe that developing countries are in a terrible situation: suffering from incredible poverty, governed by dictators and with little hope for any meaningful change. But, surprisingly, this is far from the truth. The reality is that a great transformation is occurring. Over the past 20 years, more than 700 million people have increased their income and come out of poverty. Additionally, six million fewer children die every year from disease, millions more girls are in school and millions of people have access to clean water.

Source

In an attempt to inject some positivity into their feedback, many managers rely on sandwiching negative feedback between two positive comments. However, when feedback becomes such a routine, employees can start to perceive positive feedback as simply a form of sugarcoating the negatives, thus diminishing its value. Instead, positive feedback should not simply be seen as something to cushion the negative, but should be delivered so as to reinforce and encourage good performance.

Reference 1

Some managers give feedback in a "sandwich" format. They say something good, then something bad, and then something good again. But if they do this too many times, workers may think the good things are fake. They may also think the good ones are there to hide the bad ones. This makes the good feedback weak. Positive feedback should be real. It should help workers feel proud and keep doing the good work.

Reference 2

Many managers try to make feedback softer by using the "sandwich" method: they give a positive comment, followed by a negative one, and finish with another positive. But if this happens too often, workers may think that positive words are only there to hide the bad ones. This makes positive feedback less useful. Positive feedback should not just be used to cover the negatives. It should be given clearly to support the good work.

Prediction

Many managers often share criticism between two positive comments to make it less harsh. However, if feedback is given too frequently, employees may view positive feedback as just a cover for the negatives, reducing its value. Positive feedback should not only be used to soften negative comments but also to reinforce and motivate good performance.

A fast-paced, foot-tapping rock album, Born in the USA's lyrics nevertheless carry emotional weight. Behind the catchy rock melodies that drive these powerhouse classics are stories of the dark side of the American dream. Many tracks deal with the struggles of hard-working ordinary people and the bitterness and anger they feel as life doesn't bring them riches or glory. The song Glory Days, for example, is about people in a small town looking back at when they were young and had the world at their feet while Downbound Train tells the story of a young man whose life is ruined when he loses his job. It's not hard to imagine that the artist is channelling real people he knew and the life he might have had if he hadn't become a star. The album will leave you in no doubt of the unique and extraordinary talent of the Boss.

Reference 1

Born in the USA is a rock album with fast music that makes you want to move, but the words of the songs are serious. They tell stories about the hard side of the American dream. Many songs are about working people who try hard but do not get rich or famous. For example, Glory Days is about people in a small town remembering when they were young, and Downbound Train is about a young man who loses his job and his life becomes sad. The singer seems to sing about real people he knew, and the life he might have had if he were not a star. The album shows that "the Boss" has special talent.

For example, Glory Days is about small-town people remembering their youth, while Downbound Train tells of a young man whose life falls apart after losing his job. The album showcases the exceptional and rare talent of Bruce Springsteen, often referred to as the Boss.

Lewis would like to see the number of marine protected areas in the world increase from 3 per cent to 10 per cent in an attempt to reverse the damage caused by human activity such as overfishing, polluting and littering. During his expeditions, Lewis has witnessed this environmental destruction first-hand. He's swum over coral reefs bleached by the increase in water temperature, and observed underwater deserts beneath the shallow waters of the Red Sea, devoid of life and strewn with plastic. Pugh believes that nature can recover if it is given space to do so, but the clock is ticking. If we don't start looking after our seas, we may soon have an unsolvable problem on our hands.

Reference 1

Lewis wants more parts of the sea to be protected. At the moment, only 3% is protected, and he wants this to be 10%. He hopes this will help fix the damage people cause, like too much fishing, pollution, and rubbish in the sea. On his trips, Lewis saw this damage himself. He swam over coral that had lost its colour because the water was too hot. In the Red Sea, he saw places with no fish, but plastic. Pugh believes nature can heal if we give it space, but we must do it quickly. If we do not take care of the sea now, it may soon be too late.

Are things getting worse every day? Is progress an impossible goal? In Enlightenment Now, Steven Pinker looks at the big picture of human progress and finds good news. We are living longer, healthier, freer and happier lives. The real problem, Pinker argues, is that the Enlightenment values of reason and science are under attack. When commentators and demagogues appeal to people's tribalism, fatalism and distrust, then we are in danger of causing irreparable damage to important institutions like democracy and world co-operation.

Reference 1

But Pinker also warns us. He says that clear thinking and science are under attack. Some people make people follow only their own group, lose hope, and stop trusting others. If this happens, imporatant things like democracy and countries working together may be badly damaged.

Reference 2

Are things getting worse every day? Is progress impossible? In Enlightenment Now, Steven Pinker studies the history of human progress and finds much good news. People live longer, healthier, freer, and happier lives. But Pinker warns that the real danger is the attack on Enlightenment values such as reason and science. When leaders and commentators encourage people to think only of their own group, to give up hope, or to stop trusting others, we risk damaging democracy and world co-operation in a way that can't be fixed.

The real problem, Pinker argues, is that the Enlightenment values of reason and science are under attack. In older horror films, when protagonists were in desperation, it was difficult or impossible for them to call for help or call the police. Mobile phones have made that situation a bit less believable now. What's the solution to maintain suspense? No phone coverage! If you're a hero in a horror film, it's almost certain that at a key moment, just when you absolutely need to call for help, you will not have any coverage at all. Or your phone battery will die just as you are making the call. Or both.

Reference 1

In old horror films, the main characters could not easily call for help or the police. But now people have mobile phones, so this problem looks less true. How do films keep the story scary? The answer is no phone signal! In many horror films, when the hero really needs to call, there is no signal. Or the phone battery dies while they are calling. Sometimes both happen.

Reference 2

In many horror films, at the key moment when the hero needs to call for help, there is no signal. Or the phone battery will die just as thry make the call. Or sometimes both happen.

In the 1950s, Central American commercial banana growers were facing the death of their most lucrative product, the Gros Michel banana, known as Big Mike. And now it's happening again to Big Mike's successor – the Cavendish. With its easily transported, thick-skinned and sweet-tasting fruit, the Gros Michel banana plant dominated the plantations of Central America. United Fruit, the main grower and exporter in South America at the time, mass-produced its bananas in the most efficient way possible: it cloned shoots from the stems of plants instead of growing plants from seeds, and cultivated them in densely packed fields.

Reference 1

In the 1950s, banana farmers in Central America had a big problem. Their best banana, called Gros Michel or Big Mike, was dying. Now the same problem is happening to its replacement, the Cavendish. Big Mike was very popular because it was sweet, had a thick skin, and was easy to carry and sell. United Fruit, the biggest company, grew these bananas by taking shoots from the plant stems instead of seeds. They planted the bananas very close to each other in larger fields.

Reference 2

In the 1950s, banana growers in Central America saw their most valuable banana, the Gros Michel (called Big Mike), begin to die out. Now the same problem is happening to Big Mike's replacement—the Cavendish banana. The Gros Michel was very popular because it was sweet, had a thick skin, and was easy to transport. It was the main banana grown in Central America. United Fruit, the biggest company at the time, grew bananas in the most efficient way: instead of using seeds, they cloned shoots from stems and planted then close together in larger fileds.

Prediction

In the 1950s, banana growers in Central America had a problem. Their most profitable product, the Gros Michel banana or "Big Mike," was dying. Now, the same thing is happening to Big Mike's replacement - the Cavendish. The Gros Michel banana plant was popular because its fruit was easy to transport, thick-skinned, and sweet. United Fruit, the main grower and exporter in South America at the time, produced bananas as efficiently as possible. They made shoots grow from the stems of plants instead of growing plants from seeds. They also planted many crops close together in their fields.

Spider venom is present in most species and serves the purpose of stunning or killing their prey rather than attacking humans. In fact, only 25 of the known spider species produce venom which can actually harm humans, and although spider bites can be painful, they are rarely deadly. Australia's notorious Sydney funnel-web spider has not produced any fatalities since anti-venom was developed in 1981. However, take care not to rub hairy spiders like tarantulas up the wrong way. When they feel in danger, they defend themselves by ejecting a cloud of urticating hairs. These irritating hairs then embed themselves in the skin or eyes of the attacker.

Reference 1

Most spiders have venom, but it is for catching food, not for hurting people. Only about 25 kinds of spiders can harm humans. A spider bite can be painful, but it rarely kills. In Australia, the Sydney funnel-web spider has not killed anyone since doctors made an anti-venom in 1981. But be careful with hairy spiders like tarantulas. If they feel danger, they throw out tiny hairs. These hairs can get into your skin or eyes and make them very sore.

Reference 2

Most spiders have venom, but it is mainly used to catch or kill their prey, not to attack humans. In fact, only 25 spider species are dangerous to people. Spider bites can hurt, but they almost never cause death. For example, Australia's famous Sydney funnel-web spider has not killed anyone since an anti-venom was created in 1981. You should be careful with hairy spiders like tarantulas.When they feel in danger, they release tiny sharp hairs that can stick in the skin or eyes of the attacker and cause irritation.

Prediction

Most spiders have venom to use on their prey, but they rarely harm humans. Only 25 types of spiders can hurt people, and even their bites are usually not deadly. The Sydney funnel-web spider in Australia has not caused any deaths since 1981 because of the anti-venom. Be careful with hairy spiders like tarantulas; when they feel threatened, they release hairs that can irritate your skin or eyes.

This horror film cliché was especially popular with horror films of the late 20th century. It starts with a group of teenagers all enjoying themselves, and it ends with everyone dead except one girl. At the beginning the girl is usually innocent, shy and not particularly strong. By the end, she has become the toughest and most resourceful person in the world. The last girl almost always wins in the end.

Reference 1

Reference 2

If we want to know where our capability for complex language came from, we need to look at how our brains are different from other animals. This relates to more than just brain size; it is important what other things our brains can do and when and why they evolved that way. And for this there are very few physical clues; artefacts left by our ancestors don't tell us what speech they were capable of making. One thing we can see in the remains of early humans, however, is the development of the mouth, throat and tongue. By about 100,000 years ago, humans had evolved the ability to create complex sounds. Before that, evolutionary biologists can only guess whether or not early humans communicated using more basic sounds.

Reference 1

To know how humans learned complex language, we must look at how our brains are different from animals' brains. It is not only about the size of the brain, but also about what it can do, and when and why it changes. There are not many clues, as old subjects cannot tell us what kind of speech early humans had. But we can see changes in the mouth, throat, and tongue of early human bones. Around 100,000 years ago, humans got the ability to make complex sounds. Before that, scientists could only guess if early humans used simple sounds to talk.

Reference 2

If we want to know where our ability for complex language came from, we need to look at how the human brain is different from the brains of other animals. It is not only about brain size, but also about what our brains can do, when these abilities appreared and why. There are a few physical clues about this, because objects from early humans cannot show us what kind of speech thry used. But one thing we can study is the development of the mouth, throat and tongue. Around 100,000 years ago, humans developed the ability to make complex sounds. Before that, scientists could only guess if early humans used simpler sounds to communicate.

Prediction

If we want to understand where our ability for complex language comes from, we need to look at how our brains are different from other animals. This is about more than just brain size; it's also important what other things our brains can do and when and why they evolved that way. There are very few physical clues about this, though. The things left behind by early humans don't tell us what kind of speech they could make. However, we can see that the mouth, throat, and tongue of early humans had developed by around 100,000 years ago. This means they could create complex sounds. Before that time, it's unclear if early humans communicated using more basic sounds. Evolutionary biologists can only make guesses about this.

It is society, not nature, that tells us girls should favour arts and humanities and leave maths and physics to the boys. Coupled with this is the lack of positive female role models youngsters see doing science-related jobs. Cartoons and stories often show the crazy scientist, genius inventor, or adventurous astronaut as a man. Furthermore, there is an unfortunate perception that scientists are geeky, have poor social skills or that their work is lonely and detached from the rest of the world. These are false stereotypes portrayed by the media, but they may mean that girls do not identify with scientists, and see science as an unappealing career path. If girls saw more positive female role models in science it would give them more confidence and a greater sense of belonging in those subjects.

Reference 1

Society tells us that girls should choose arts and humanities, and boys should choose maths and physics. Another problem is that children do not often see women working in science. In cartoons and stories, the scientist, inventor, or astronaut is usually a man. People also think scientists are strange, not good at talking to others, or always working alone. These ideas are not true, but they can make girls feel that science is not for them. If girls saw more women in science, they would feel more confident and feel that they belong in those subjects.

Reference 2

It is society, not nature, that suggests girls should study arts and humanities while boys study maths and physics. There is also a negative idea that scientists are "geeky," have poor social skills, or work alone, far from the real world. These are false stereotypes shown by the media, but they may stop girls from seeing themselves as scientists and make science seem unattractive. If girls saw more positive female working in science, it would give them more confidence and help them feel that they belong in those subjects.

Prediction

Society, not nature, tells girls to prefer arts and humanities and leave math and physics to boys. These stereotypes may prevent girls from identifying with scientists and seeing science as an attractive career path. If girls saw more positive female role models in science, they would feel more confident and have a stronger sense of belonging in those subjects.

So much of what the speaker said rang true. I honestly believe there's a danger that the more connected we are, the more isolated we feel. I don't think this is such an issue for my generation who've lived without technology for so long. We know how to be alone and, more importantly, we know that it's OK to be alone. But the under 20s are another kettle of fish. They're so busy communicating that they never experience the feeling of solitude and run the risk of not learning how to enjoy their own company. In addition, they're learning conversation through messages that can be edited and changed at the expense of learning the art of real conversation in real time with the person in front of you.

Reference 1

I agreed with much of what the speaker said. I think that when people are more connected, they can sometimes feel lonelier. For my generation, this is not such a big problem, because we lived many years without modern technology. We know how to be alone, and we know it is OK. But for people under 20, it is different. They spend so much time sending messages that they do not feel real solitude. They may not learn how to enjoy being alone. They also talk by messages that can be changed, instead of learning face-to-face conversations.

Reference 2

Much of what the speaker said felt true to me. I believe there is a danger that the more connected we are, the more lonely we may feel. For my generation, this is less of a problem because we lived for many years without technology. We know how to spend time alone, and we know it is OK to be alone. But young people under 20 are different. They are always busy communicating, but they never really experience solitude. This means they may not learn how to enjoy being alone. Also, they are learning to talk through messages that can be edited, instead of learning real conversation in real time with another person in front of you.

Prediction

The speaker's words resonated with me. I believe that as we become more connected, there is a risk of feeling more isolated. This may not be as true for my generation, as we have lived without technology for a long time and know how to be alone, which is okay. However, the under 20s face a different situation. They are so preoccupied with communication that they might miss out on the experience of solitude and the ability to enjoy their own company. Furthermore, they often learn conversation through messages that can be edited, rather than experiencing real-time conversations with the person in front of them.

Row 99 | ID 120 | SARI: 42.770 | BERTScore-F1: 0.914 | MeaningBERT: 85.870 | FKGL: 9.080 | FRE: 59.040 | stop

list_markers_present, two_or_more_list_markers

Source

Don't always follow positive feedback with negative feedback. When positive and negative feedback always appear to go hand in hand, the positives can become devalued and ignored. Ensure there are times when positive feedback is given for its own sake and resist the temptation to offer constructive criticism.

Reference 1

Do not always say something bad after saying something good. If good and bad comments always come together, the good words may not feel real. Sometimes, give good feedback only, and try not to add any criticism.

Reference 2

Don't always give negative feedback after positive feedback. If positive and negative comments always come together, the positive ones can lose their value and be ignored. Make sure you give positive feedback on its own, and avoid the urge to add criticism.

Prediction

1. Give positive feedback on its own instead of always pairing it with negative feedback.
2. Do this by resisting the urge to give constructive criticism when you're giving positive feedback.
3. This helps keep the value of positive feedback high, so people will pay more attention to it.
